Calculate the angles marked $x$ and $y$ in the diagram below:

![merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-4-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-4-subquestion-1-00.png "merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-4-subquestion-1-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a quadrilateral} = 360 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 133             &= 180 \\\\
x                   &= 180 - 133 \\\\
x                   &= 47 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $x$ to find $y$.

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 90 + y + 100    &= 360 \\\\
47 + 90 + y + 100   &= 360 \\\\
y + 237             &= 360 \\\\
y                   &= 360 - 237 \\\\
y                   &= 123 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

x $=$ $47 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

y $=$ $123 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

$

<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate the angles marked $p, q, r, s$ and $t$ in the diagram below:

![merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-4-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-4-subquestion-2-00.png "merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-4-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$


ABBR: $\text{Angles around a point add up to} = 360 \degree$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
p + 48  &= 180 \\\\
p       &= 180 - 48 \\\\
p       &= 132 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

As it is an isosceles triangle, angle $r= q$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
q + 48 + r  &= 180 \\\\
q + q       &= 180 - 48 \\\\
2 q         &= 132 \\\\
q           &= \dfrac {132} {2} \\\\
q           &= \dfrac {66 \times 2} {2} \\\\
q           &= \dfrac {66 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
q           &= 66 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$r = q = 66 \degree$

As $r$ and $t$ are opposite angles,

$t = r = 66 \degree$

Similarly,

$u$ will be equal to $s$

As we know, the sum of a complete angle is $360 \degree$. So,

$
\begin{aligned}
u + r + s + t    &= 360 \\\\
s + 66 + s + 66  &= 360 \\\\
2 s + 132        &= 360 \\\\
2 s              &= 360 - 132 \\\\
2 s              &= 228 \\\\
s                &= \dfrac {228} {2} \\\\
s                &= \dfrac {114 \times 2} {2} \\\\
s                &= \dfrac {114 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
s                &= 114 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

$s = u = 114 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

p $=$ $132 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

q $=$ $66 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

r $=$ $66 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

s $=$ $114 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

t $=$ $66 \degree$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

u $=$ $114 \degree$

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sean scored $27$ out of $45$ in a test. Write this as a percentage.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Percentage} &= \dfrac {27} {45} \times 100 \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 100} {45} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 3 \times 5 \times 20} {9 \times 5} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 3 \times \cancel {5} \times 20} {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \times 20 \\\\
                   &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Percentage} &= \dfrac {27} {45} \times 100 \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 100} {45} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 3 \times 5 \times 20} {9 \times 5} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 3 \times \cancel {5} \times 20} {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \times 20 \\\\
                   &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In a class of $30$ pupils, $21$ are girls. What percentage of the class is boys?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boys}      &= 30 - 21 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\
\text {\% of boys}          &= \dfrac {9} {30} \times 100 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {3} \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \%$

</div>

                            &= 9 \\\\

                            &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {3} \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rearrange the following in order of size, smallest to largest.

$\dfrac{9}{25}, \quad 0.371, \quad \dfrac{2}{5}, \quad 38\%$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad 38 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {25 \times 4}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2 \times 2} {5 \times 2},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {100}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {4} {10},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371,  \quad 0.4,  \quad 0.38
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371, \quad 0.38, \quad 0.4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 
<emptyline>
Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad 38 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {25 \times 4}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2 \times 2} {5 \times 2},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {100}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {4} {10},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371,  \quad 0.4,  \quad 0.38
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's put them in ascending order now.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371, \quad 0.38, \quad 0.4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='question question'>

A gardener measures the night time temperatures over two evenings and records the results in the table shown below:

|                  | Monday Temperature in degrees Centigrade     | Tuesday Temperature in degree Centigrade     |
|:----------------:|:--------------------------------------------:|:--------------------------------------------:|
| $ 10\ \text{pm}$ |                      $3$                     |                      $2$                     |
| $ 11\ \text{pm}$ |                      $3$                     |                      $1$                     |
| $ 12\ \text{am}$ |                      $2$                     |                      $1$                     |
| $ 1\ \text{am}$  |                      $1$                     |                      $0$                     |
| $ 2\ \text{am}$  |                      $0$                     |                     $-1$                     |
| $ 3\ \text{am}$  |                     $-2$                     |                     $-3$                     |
| $ 4\ \text{am}$  |                     $-1$                     |                     $-1$                     |
| $ 5\ \text{am}$  |                      $0$                     |                      $0$                     |
| $ 6\ \text{am}$  |                      $1$                     |                      $2$                     |
| $ 7\ \text{am}$  |                      $2$                     |                      $4$                     |

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3x &= 36 \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {36} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times 3} {3} \\\\
x  &= \dfrac {12 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
x  &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$12$

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

The perimeter of a rectangle is $22 \ \text{cm}$. One of the sides has length $8 \ \text{cm}$. 
Calculate the lengths of the other three sides.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the width $= w$ 

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of rectangle}      &= 22 \\\\
2 \times (8 + w)                    &= 22 \\\\
16 + 2 \times w                     &= 22 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 22 - 16 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 6 \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
w                                   &= 3 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Other three sides of rectangle are 3cm, 3cm, 8cm.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {3cm, \ 3cm, \ 8cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle
<emptyline>
Let the width $= w$ 
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of rectangle}      &= 22 \\\\
2 \times (8 + w)                    &= 22 \\\\
16 + 2 \times w                     &= 22 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 22 - 16 \\\\
2 \times \text w                    &= 6 \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {6} {2} \\\\
w                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
w                                   &= 3 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Other three sides of rectangle are 3cm, 3cm, 8cm.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {3cm, \ 3cm, \ 8cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The contents of part of a wardrobe are shown in the pie chart below:

![merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-14-00.png](/assets/merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-14-00.png "merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-14-00.png")

What fraction, in its simplest form, of footwear are boots? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fraction of Boots}       &= \dfrac {\text {Boots}} {\text {Total Footwear}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {63+9}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {72}      \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{9}} {8 \times \cancel{9}}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}   
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {8}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Fraction of Boots}       &= \dfrac {\text {Boots}} {\text {Total Footwear}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {63+9}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {9} {72}      \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{9}} {8 \times \cancel{9}}    \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {8}   
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {8}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the highest common factor (HCF) of $66$ and $30$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 66, 30 are:}\\\\
66&=2\times3\times11\\\\
30&=2\times 3\times5\\\\
\text{The factors that given numbers have in common are:}\\\\
&=2\times3\\\\
&=6
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

6

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 66, 30 are:}\\\\
66&=2\times3\times11\\\\
30&=2\times 3\times5\\\\
\text{The factors that given numbers have in common are:}\\\\
&=2\times3\\\\
&=6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>6
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the lowest common multiple (LCM) of $12$ and $8$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 12, 8 are:}\\\\
12&=2\times2\times3\\\\
8&=2\times 2\times2\\\\
\text{Multiply each factor the greater number of times}\\
\text{it occurs in steps i) or ii) above to find the LCM:}\\\\
&=2\times2\times2\times 3\\\\
&=24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

24

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 12, 8 are:}\\\\
12&=2\times2\times3\\\\
8&=2\times 2\times2\\\\
\text{Multiply each factor the greater number of times}\\
\text{it occurs in steps i) or ii) above to find the LCM:}\\\\
&=2\times2\times2\times 3\\\\
&=24
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>24
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Fazal has $\pounds 2.60$ and Gareth has $\pounds 5.30$. Gareth gives Fazal some $5 \ \text{p}$ coins. 
Each boy now has the same amount of money. How many $5 \ \text{p}$ coins did Gareth give Fazal?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let number of coins $= a$

Money given $= a \times 5 \ \text{p}$ 

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 5.3 - 5 \ \text{p} \times a                     &= \pounds 2.6 + 5\ \text{p} \times a \\\\
5.3 \times 100\ \text{p} - 5\ \text{p} \times a           &= 2.6 \times 100\ \text{p} + 5\ \text{p} \times a \\\\
530 \ \text{p} - 5 \ \text{p} \times a                    &= 260\ \text{p} + 5\ \text{p} \times a \\\\
530 - 5 \times a                                      &= 260 + 5 \times a \\\\
530 - 5a                                              &= 260 + 5a \\\\
530 - 260                                             &= 5a + 5a \\\\
5a + 5a                                               &= 530 - 260 \\\\
10a                                                   &= 270 \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{270}{10} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{27\cancel{0}{1\cancel{0}} \\\\
a                                                     &= 27
\end{aligned}
$

Number of coins = 27

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$27$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let number of coins $= a$
<emptyline>
Money given $= a \times 5 \ \text{p}$ 
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\pounds 5.3 - 5 \ \text{p} \times a                     &= \pounds 2.6 + 5\ \text{p} \times a \\\\
5.3 \times 100\ \text{p} - 5\ \text{p} \times a           &= 2.6 \times 100\ \text{p} + 5\ \text{p} \times a \\\\
530 \ \text{p} - 5 \ \text{p} \times a                    &= 260\ \text{p} + 5\ \text{p} \times a \\\\
530 - 5 \times a                                      &= 260 + 5 \times a \\\\
530 - 5a                                              &= 260 + 5a \\\\
530 - 260                                             &= 5a + 5a \\\\
5a + 5a                                               &= 530 - 260 \\\\
10a                                                   &= 270 \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{270}{10} \\\\
a                                                     &= \dfrac{27\cancel{0}{1\cancel{0}} \\\\
a                                                     &= 27
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of coins = 27
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$27$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 3 \times 2^3 \\\\
&= 3 \times 8 \\\\
&= 24
\end{aligned}

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 2 \times 3^2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

If: $\dfrac{3}{x} \times \dfrac{4}{7} = \dfrac{3}{14}$ 

Calculate the value of $x$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {x} \times \dfrac {4} {7}  &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {3 \times 4} {x \times 7}      &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {x}                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3 \times 4 \times 14} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 14} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7 \times 2} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {7}} \\\\  
x                                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {3} {x} \times \dfrac {4} {7}  &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {3 \times 4} {x \times 7}      &= \dfrac {3} {14} \\\\
\dfrac {1} {x}                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 7} {3 \times 4 \times 14} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 14} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 7 \times 2} {3 \times 7} \\\\
x                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {7} \times 2} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {7}} \\\\  
x                                     &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

There are $x$ horses, $y$ cows and one three legged dog in a field.

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down an expression in algebra for the total number of legs in the field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of legs} &= x \times 4 + y \times 4 + 3 \\\\
                            &= 4x + 4y + 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4x + 4y + 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of legs} &= x \times 4 + y \times 4 + 3 \\\\
                            &= 4x + 4y + 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4x + 4y + 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Half of the cows and one quarter of the horses leave the field. 
Write down an expression in algebra for the number of heads that remain in the field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of heads}                        &= x + y + 1 - ( \dfrac{1}{2} \times x ) - ( \dfrac{1}{4} \times y ) \\\\
                                                    &= x - \dfrac{x}{2} + y - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x \times 2}{1 \times 2} - \dfrac{x \times 1}{2 \times 1} + \dfrac{y \times 4}{1 \times 4} - \dfrac{y \times 1}{4 \times 1} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x}{2} - \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{4y}{4} - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x-x}{2} + \dfrac{4y - y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of heads}                        &= x + y + 1 - ( \dfrac{1}{2} \times x ) - ( \dfrac{1}{4} \times y ) \\\\
                                                    &= x - \dfrac{x}{2} + y - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x \times 2}{1 \times 2} - \dfrac{x \times 1}{2 \times 1} + \dfrac{y \times 4}{1 \times 4} - \dfrac{y \times 1}{4 \times 1} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x}{2} - \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{4y}{4} - \dfrac{y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{2x-x}{2} + \dfrac{4y - y}{4} + 1 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac{x}{2} + \dfrac{3y}{4} + 1$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question question'>

An alloy contains iron and tungsten in the ratio $5$ parts to $1$ part.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the mass of the alloy is $72 \ \text{kg}$, how much iron and tungsten does it contain?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of iron $= a$

Mass of tungsten $= 72 - a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{kg} : 1 \ \text{kg}                   &= a \ \text{kg} : (72 - a) \ \text{kg} \\\\
\dfrac{5}{1}                                    &= \dfrac {a} {72 - a} \\\\
5 \times (72 - a)                               &= a \\\\
5 \times 72 - 5a                                &= a \\\\
360 - 5a                                        &= a \\\\
360                                             &= 5a + a \\\\
5a + a                                          &= 360 \\\\
6a                                              &= 360 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 60} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 60} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of iron}         &= 60 \ \text{kg} \\\\
\text{Mass of tungsten}     &= 72 - 60 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using whole to part ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{alloy}                  &:& \text{iron}                &:& \text{tungsten} \\\\
= & (5 + 1) \ \text{kg}           &:& 5 \ \text{kg}              &:& 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 6                             &:& 5                          &:& 1 \\\\
= & 6 \times 12                   &:& 5 \times 12                &:& 1 \times 12 \\\\
= & 72 \ \text{kg}                &:& 60 \ \text{kg}             &:& 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

Mass of iron $= 60 \ \text{kg}$

Mass of tungsten $= 12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \ \text{kg}, 12 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the mass of iron $= a$
<emptyline>
Mass of tungsten $= 72 - a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{kg} : 1 \ \text{kg}                   &= a \ \text{kg} : (72 - a) \ \text{kg} \\\\
\dfrac{5}{1}                                    &= \dfrac {a} {72 - a} \\\\
5 \times (72 - a)                               &= a \\\\
5 \times 72 - 5a                                &= a \\\\
360 - 5a                                        &= a \\\\
360                                             &= 5a + a \\\\
5a + a                                          &= 360 \\\\
6a                                              &= 360 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {360} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 60} {6} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 60} {\cancel 6} \\\\
a                                               &= 60 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of iron}         &= 60 \ \text{kg} \\\\
\text{Mass of tungsten}     &= 72 - 60 \\\\
                            &= 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using whole to part ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{alloy}                  &:& \text{iron}                &:& \text{tungsten} \\\\
= & (5 + 1) \ \text{kg}           &:& 5 \ \text{kg}              &:& 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 6                             &:& 5                          &:& 1 \\\\
= & 6 \times 12                   &:& 5 \times 12                &:& 1 \times 12 \\\\
= & 72 \ \text{kg}                &:& 60 \ \text{kg}             &:& 12 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of iron $= 60 \ \text{kg}$
<emptyline>
Mass of tungsten $= 12 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \ \text{kg}, 12 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If there is $15 \ \text{kg}$ of iron in a quantity of the alloy, how much tungsten is there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of tungsten $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \ \text{kg} : 1 \ \text{kg}                   &= 15 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{kg} \\\\
5                                               &= \dfrac {15} {a} \\\\
5 \times a                                      &= 15 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                               &= 3 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Tungsten $= 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{iron}                                 &:& \text{tungsten} \\\\
= & 5 \ \text{kg}                               &:& 1 \ \text{kg} \\\\
= & 1                                           &:& \dfrac {1} {5} \\\\
= & 1 \times 15                                 &:& \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
= & 15                                          &:& \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5} \\\\
= & 15                                          &:& \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 15 \ \text{kg}                              &:& 3 \ \text{kg}
\end{array}
$

Tungsten $= 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

\end{array}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A large cube is made by gluing together smaller white and grey cubes of volume $1 \ \text{cm}^3$ as shown. 
If five grey cubes are removed from each face of the larger cube, what is the total volume remaining?

![merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-23-00.png](/assets/merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-23-00.png "merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-23-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Volume of a cube} = (\text{Side}) ^ { 3 }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Volume of the big cube}       &= 5 \times 5 \times 5 \\\\
                                    &= 125 \ \text{cm}^3 \\\\
\text{Volume of cubes to remove}    &= 6 \times 1 \times 1 \times 1 \\\\
                                    &= 6 \ \text{cm}^3 \\\\
\text{Remaining Volume}             &= 125 - 30 \\\\
                                    &= 95 \ \text{cm}^3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$95 \ \text{cm}^3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_volume_cube
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Volume of the big cube}       &= 5 \times 5 \times 5 \\\\
                                    &= 125 \ \text{cm}^3 \\\\
\text{Volume of cubes to remove}    &= 6 \times 1 \times 1 \times 1 \\\\
                                    &= 6 \ \text{cm}^3 \\\\
\text{Remaining Volume}             &= 125 - 30 \\\\
                                    &= 95 \ \text{cm}^3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$95 \ \text{cm}^3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

If a basketball weighs $300 \ \text{g}$ plus half its own weight, how much does it weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the weight of basketball $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
b                                                 &= 300 + \dfrac {b} {2} \\\\
b - \dfrac {b} {2}                                &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b \times 2} {1 \times 2} - \dfrac {b} {2} &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b} {2} - \dfrac {b} {2}                 &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b - b} {2}                              &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b} {2}                                    &= 300 \\\\
b                                                 &= 300 \times 2 \\\\
b                                                 &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the weight of basketball $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
b                                                 &= 300 + \dfrac {b} {2} \\\\
b - \dfrac {b} {2}                                &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b \times 2} {1 \times 2} - \dfrac {b} {2} &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b} {2} - \dfrac {b} {2}                 &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {2 b - b} {2}                              &= 300 \\\\
\dfrac {b} {2}                                    &= 300 \\\\
b                                                 &= 300 \times 2 \\\\
b                                                 &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$600 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

$25$ students have taken an exam and the mean number of marks is recorded as $83$. 

The examiner subsequently awards $5$ additional marks to $9$ of the students and takes away $2$ marks from $10$ other students. 

What will the new mean mark be?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total marks earlier}              &= 25 \times 83 \\\\
                                        &= 2075 \\\\
\text{Total marks later}                &= 2075 + 9 \times 5 - 10 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 2075 + 45 - 20 \\\\
                                        &= 2100 \\\\
\text{New Mean}                         &= \dfrac {2100} {25} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 84} {25} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 84} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                        &= 84
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$84$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total marks earlier}              &= 25 \times 83 \\\\
                                        &= 2075 \\\\
\text{Total marks later}                &= 2075 + 9 \times 5 - 10 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 2075 + 45 - 20 \\\\
                                        &= 2100 \\\\
\text{New Mean}                         &= \dfrac {2100} {25} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {25 \times 84} {25} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 84} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                                        &= 84
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$84$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Ants at the end of the second day} &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
&= 20 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

20

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

\begin{aligned}

<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Find in terms of $x$ the perimeter of the following triangle:

![merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-28-00.png](/assets/merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-28-00.png "merchant-taylors-school--11-plus--maths--9999--practice-paper-4/section-1-question-28-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of triangle} &= (4x - 3) + (2x + 1) + (5 - 2x) \\\\
                              &= 4x + 2x - 2x - 3 + 1 + 5 \\\\
                              &= 4x + 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4x + 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of triangle} &= (4x - 3) + (2x + 1) + (5 - 2x) \\\\
                              &= 4x + 2x - 2x - 3 + 1 + 5 \\\\
                              &= 4x + 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4x + 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

An egg box holds $6$ eggs. How many boxes are needed for $100$ eggs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boxes needed}      &= \dfrac {100} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {50 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2} \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {50} {3} \\\\
                                    &= 16 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
                                    &\approx 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boxes needed}      &= \dfrac {100} {6} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {50 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2} \times 3} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {50} {3} \\\\
                                    &= 16 \text{ remainder } 2 \\\\
                                    &\approx 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A toy train travels $6 \ \text{metres}$ in two seconds. How far will it travel in one minute?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance travelled in one minute}     &= \dfrac {6} {2} \times 60 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {6 \times 60} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 60} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 60} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                            &= 3 \times 60 \\\\
                                            &= 180 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$180 \ \text {metres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 60} {2} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 3 \times 60} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                            &= 3 \times 60 \\\\
                                            &= 180 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

One of the angles of an isosceles triangle is $96 \degree$. Find the sizes of the other two angles.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


Let the missing angle $= a$

As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle is also $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 96      &= 180  \\\\
2a + 96         &= 180  \\\\
2a              &= 180 - 96  \\\\
2a              &= 84  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {84} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
a               &= 42 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Other two angles $= 42 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

2a              &= 180 - 96  \\\\
2a              &= 84  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {84} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times 2} {2}  \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {42 \times \cancel{2}}{\cancel{2}}   \\\\
a               &= 42 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Other two angles $= 42 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$42 \degree$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

Mrs Jones is $24 \ \text{years}$ older than her daughter. The sum of their ages is $70 \ \text{years}$. How old is Mrs Jones?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Mrs Jones age $=a$

Her daughter's age $= a - 24$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a - 24 &= 70 \\\\
2 a        &= 70 + 24 \\\\
2 a        &= 94 \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {94} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a          &= 47 \ \text {years}
\end{aligned}
$

Mrs Jones age $= 47 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$47 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Mrs Jones age $=a$
<emptyline>
Her daughter's age $= a - 24$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a - 24 &= 70 \\\\
2 a        &= 70 + 24 \\\\
2 a        &= 94 \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {94} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {47 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a          &= 47 \ \text {years}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mrs Jones age $= 47 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A metal rod is $10 \dfrac{4}{5} \ \text{metres}$ long.  How many short rods $\dfrac{3}{10} \ \text{metres}$ 
long can be cut from the longer rod?  

Show full working

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Numbers of rods} &= \dfrac {\text {Length of longer rod}} {\text {Length of shorter rod}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \dfrac {4} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\dfrac {54} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {5} \times \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54 \times 10} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 18 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 18 \times \cancel {5} \times 2} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                        &= 18 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Numbers of rods} &= \dfrac {\text {Length of longer rod}} {\text {Length of shorter rod}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {10 \dfrac {4} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\dfrac {54} {5}} {\dfrac {3} {10}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54} {5} \times \dfrac {10} {3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {54 \times 10} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 18 \times 5 \times 2} {5 \times 3} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 18 \times \cancel {5} \times 2} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                        &= 18 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<!-- - if !options[:questions_only] -->
<!-- = render :reviewsubtag, question: question -->
<div class='question question'>

A "double-decker" sandwich has three slices of bread and two layers of filling. 
(e.g. bread/filling/bread/filling/bread). 
Each slice of bread has to be buttered on each side that is in contact with the filling. 
I make as many of these sandwiches as possible from a sliced loaf which has $22$ usable slices, 
excluding crusts which are not used. How many sides of bread do I have to butter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

For every sandwich, $4$ sides of bread need to be buttered.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sandwiches}    &= 22 \div 3 \\\\
                               &= 7 \ \ \text{remainder} \ \ 1 \\\\
                               &\approx 7 \\\\
\text{Number of sides}         &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
                               &= 28  
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>For every sandwich, $4$ sides of bread need to be buttered.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sandwiches}    &= 22 \div 3 \\\\
                               &= 7 \ \ \text{remainder} \ \ 1 \\\\
                               &\approx 7 \\\\
\text{Number of sides}         &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
                               &= 28  
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
&                          &   4                               &  \boxed{\phantom{15}}     \\
\times &                   &   \boxed{\phantom{15}}            &  \boxed{\phantom{15}}     \\
\hline
                          &    \boxed{\phantom{15}}           &   8                        &   \boxed{\phantom{15}}    \\
                          &         8                         &   \boxed{\phantom{15}}     &   0   \\
\hline
\boxed{\phantom{15}}      &    \boxed{\phantom{15}}           &   4   &   *
\end{array}
$
